## [0] correct=True · `New_York_City_Bar_Association_Model_Form_of_Non-Disclosure_Agreement_2015.txt`

- request_id: `9efc2a406a58`
- error_type: `none`

### Query

Consider New York City Bar Association's Non-Disclosure Agreement; Does the document allow the Receiving Party to share some Confidential Information with their employees?

### Gold

(e) “Representatives” means, with respect to any Person, the Affiliates of such Person and any of its and their respective directors, officers, employees, managing members, general partners, attorneys, accountants, investment bankers, financial advisors, consultants and other advisors[, and any actual or potential sources of debt financing for such Person or its Affiliates]12; provided that, for the avoidance of doubt, in no event shall any actual or potential sources of equity financing for Buyer or its Affiliates be considered a “Representative” of Buyer for purposes of this Agreement without the prior written consent of the Company.
Buyer agrees that Buyer and its Representatives shall
(iii) disclose Evaluation Material only to Representatives of Buyer to whom disclosure is needed to facilitate the evaluation, negotiation and/or consummation of the Transaction.

### RAG Answer

Yes. In the New York City Bar Association Model Form Non-Disclosure Agreement, the Receiving Party is referred to as the **Buyer**, and the confidential information is generally referred to as **Evaluation Material**.

Under **Section 2(a)**, the Buyer may disclose Evaluation Material to its **Representatives**, but only to the extent disclosure is needed to facilitate the evaluation, negotiation, and/or consummation of the Transaction.

“**Representatives**” is defined in **Section 1(e)** to include, among others, the Buyer’s **directors, officers, employees**, attorneys, accountants, consultants, and other advisors.

Therefore, the Buyer may share some Evaluation Material with its employees, provided that:

1. The employees are acting as Buyer’s Representatives;  
2. The disclosure is necessary to facilitate the Transaction; and  
3. Before giving access, the Buyer or one of its Representatives informs the employee of the Agreement’s terms and instructs them to comply with the applicable confidentiality obligations.

The Buyer is also generally liable for breaches by its Representatives, except where the Representative has signed a separate joinder agreement or a separate confidentiality agreement acceptable to the Company.

### Judge

- reason: 系统回答覆盖了金标关键要点：Representatives 包含 employees，且可向其为交易目的需要的 Representatives 披露 Confidential/Evaluation Material；额外扩写不构成错误。
- gap: 无

Buyer agrees that Buyer and its Representatives shall (i) use the Evaluation Material solely for the purpose of evaluating, negotiating and consummating the Transaction (or other legal, audit or compliance purposes permitted by this Agreement);13
(iii) Notwithstanding the foregoing and for the avoidance of doubt, none of Buyer or any of its Representatives shall be permitted to make any disclosure of Evaluation Material or Discussions Disclosure if Buyer or any of its Representatives has, without the prior written consent of the Company’s board of directors, initiated any proxy contest, tender offer, other effort to enter into a business combination with the Company, or any plan or proposal described in Item 4 of Schedule 13D, or taken any other action in violation of Section 8, that would reasonably be expected to trigger such requirement of disclosure.
Neither this Agreement nor any of the rights or obligations hereunder may be assigned by any party without the prior written consent of the non-assigning party.
Except with respect to the matters specifically set forth herein, neither party shall have any rights or obligations of any kind whatsoever with respect to a Transaction by virtue of this Agreement or any other written or oral expression by the parties or their respective Representatives unless and until a Definitive Transaction Agreement is executed and delivered.

### RAG Answer

Yes. Under Section 2(a) of the New York City Bar Association Model Form of Non-Disclosure Agreement, the Buyer and its Representatives must use the confidential information—defined in the document as “Evaluation Material”—**solely for the purpose of evaluating, negotiating, and consummating the Transaction**, or for other legal, audit, or compliance purposes permitted by the Agreement.

The Agreement also restricts disclosure of Evaluation Material to only those Representatives who need it to facilitate the evaluation, negotiation, or consummation of the Transaction.

### Query

Consider the Non-Disclosure Agreement between University of Wisconsin Oshkosh; Does the document specify whether the Receiving Party is required to destroy or return Confidential Information upon the termination of the Agreement?

### Gold

4. The Receiving Party will return or destroy Confidential Information provided by the Disclosing Party upon termination of the Agreement.

### RAG Answer

Yes. Section 4 of the Non-Disclosure Agreement specifies that the Receiving Party “will return or destroy Confidential Information provided by the Disclosing Party upon termination of the Agreement.”

It also provides limited exceptions: the Receiving Party’s designated representative may retain one copy of all Confidential Information to address potential claims and to comply with legal or recordkeeping requirements, and neither party is obligated to destroy Confidential Information stored electronically on backup systems or computer hard drives after a file is deleted. However, any retained electronic information remains subject to the confidentiality obligations of the Agreement.

Section 6 also states that Confidential Information will be provided in writing and marked “Confidential.”

Therefore, information that is not expressly marked or designated as “Confidential” by the Disclosing Party is not treated as Confidential Information under the agreement.

### RAG Answer

Under Section 1(c) of the Non-Disclosure Agreement, “Confidential Information” **does not include information that is independently discovered by an employee, agent, or representative of the Receiving Party who had no knowledge of the Confidential Information disclosed**.
